{"forms":[{"form":"Euclid's Lemma","tags":["alternative"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"glosses":["The proposition that if a prime number p divides an arbitrary product ab of integers, then p divides a or b or both;"],"tags":["uncountable"],"topics":["mathematics","number-theory","sciences"]},{"glosses":["The proposition that if a prime number p divides an arbitrary product ab of integers, then p divides a or b or both;\nslightly more generally, the proposition that for integers a, b, c, if a divides bc and gcd(a, b) = 1, then a divides c;\n(algebra, by generalisation) the proposition that for elements a, b, c of a given principal ideal domain, if a divides bc and gcd(a, b) = 1, then a divides c.","slightly more generally, the proposition that for integers a, b, c, if a divides bc and gcd(a, b) = 1, then a divides c;"],"tags":["uncountable"],"topics":["mathematics","number-theory","sciences"]},{"glosses":["The proposition that if a prime number p divides an arbitrary product ab of integers, then p divides a or b or both;\nslightly more generally, the proposition that for integers a, b, c, if a divides bc and gcd(a, b) = 1, then a divides c;\n(algebra, by generalisation) the proposition that for elements a, b, c of a given principal ideal domain, if a divides bc and gcd(a, b) = 1, then a divides c.","the proposition that for elements a, b, c of a given principal ideal domain, if a divides bc and gcd(a, b) = 1, then a divides c."],"tags":["uncountable"],"topics":["algebra","mathematics","number-theory","sciences"]}],"upstream_line":8006324,"upstream_offset":18725117388,"upstream_record_sha256":"dc7aee1991e51a8cc11ed30f7a2608de0d1691e23b4b4ea3f16a1f015c2e918e","word":"Euclid's lemma"}
